| 2026-06-09 | self | I again wrote a Steel wrapper with `define` inside a conditional `begin`, triggering `BadSyntax`. | Keep Steel command wrappers either fully top-level or use `let` for branch-local bindings; do not put `define` inside conditional branches. |
| 2026-06-09 | self | My first background Nix wrapper used `mkdir ... && (nix ...) & echo $! > target/pi/...`; shell precedence backgrounded the whole `&&` chain, so `echo` raced before `mkdir` created the directory. | For background validation wrappers, run setup with semicolons first, then background only the build subshell: `mkdir -p target/pi; rm ...; (nix build ...; echo $? > status) & pid=$!; echo $pid > pid`. |
| 2026-06-09 | self | I tried to `wait` directly on the `spawn-process` result again, even though this environment returns `(Ok child)` and the napkin already documents the wrapper shape. | Always unwrap `spawn-process` with `Ok->value` before `(wait child)` in Steel process snippets. |
| 2026-06-10 | self | I again guessed the Steel process API, first passing multiple args to `spawn-process`, then asserting `(wait child)` equals integer `0` even though it returns `(Ok 0)`. | Use `(command "prog" (list ...))`, pass that to `spawn-process`, unwrap the child with `Ok->value`, and inspect/print `(wait child)` as `(Ok status)` unless writing a known-good matcher. |
| 2026-06-10 | self | While migrating canonical content-ref validators, tightening operator dogfood refs exposed sentinel strings like `blake3:missing-signed-key` that looked ref-shaped but were not canonical and broke release evidence tests. | Use deterministic synthetic canonical refs (e.g. `dogfood_ref("missing-...")`) for missing-ref placeholders when a record field is semantically a content ref; keep human-readable missing state in decision/diagnostics fields. |

## User Preferences
- Prefer explicit Nickel and Steel contract boundaries where applicable: Nickel for static declarative policy/config/schema gates, Steel only for reviewed dynamic predicates/trusted callables, both enforced through Basalt before side effects.
- For the Octet disabled-lint burn-down, prefer a faster inner loop: targeted refactor, `cargo fmt`, focused tests/checks, a no-disabled Octet probe, and immediate `dylint.toml` restore; defer full workspace/lib Octet evidence, Cairn gates, docs refresh, Nix/dogfood, and larger validation until larger accepted batches or explicit checkpoint requests.

- 2026-06-11 Goblins vat runtime first slices: keep local vat/object fixture under `src/runtime/vat.rs`, emit `vat-fixture-run-v1` binding existing Trellis runtime predicate receipts for near/far calls, actormap commit/rollback, promise pipeline, and revocation cleanup. Snapshot fixture emits `vat-snapshot-v1` binding snapshot authority predicates for admitted vs claimed authority refs. Restore fixture emits `vat-object-upgrade-recipe-v1` plus `vat-restore-receipt-v1` pass/missing-recipe denial for deterministic object schema upgrades. Promise fixture emits `vat-promise-fixture-v1` over promise-state and promise-pipeline predicate receipts for resolved, broken, cancelled, timeout, terminal-mutation denial, and stale-pipeline cleanup. Ambient authority fixture emits `vat-ambient-authority-fixture-v1` over object-authority predicate receipts showing newly spawned objects have no filesystem/network/clock/process/dataspace/store/blob/consensus/choreography/host-resource authority unless explicitly endowed and admitted. Rights fixture emits `vat-rights-fixture-v1` over rights-amplification predicate receipts: matching private brand recovers sealed authority; wrong brand and over-broad recovery deny. Distributed ref fixture emits `vat-distributed-ref-fixture-v1` over distributed-ref-lifetime predicate receipts for live session use, disconnect cleanup, admitted handoff replacement, stale descriptor denial, and pending-call failure requirements. These are local evidence only and should not claim Goblins/OCapN/CapTP compatibility or grant transport/authority/policy/resource/provenance trust.
- 2026-06-10 Trellis snapshot authority slice: `RuntimeSnapshotAuthorityState` can stay bounded-local with canonical sorted snapshot/admitted/claimed/requested/readable/redacted refs. Validate claimed authority refs are a subset of admitted authority refs, readable assertion refs are admitted, requested refs are either readable or redacted, and readable/redacted sets do not overlap. For malformed snapshot refs, omit the invalid snapshot ref from receipt `state_refs` but preserve a deny diagnostic so harness receipt parsing still accepts the receipt.
- 2026-06-10 Trellis near/far refs slice: model `RuntimeNearFarRefState` with canonical `reference_ref`, near/far kind, liveness, caller/target vat IDs, and sync/async call mode. Allow sync only for live near refs in the same vat; deny cross-vat near refs, synchronous far refs, dead refs, empty vat IDs, and noncanonical refs. Keep runtime predicate state refs canonical by omitting an invalid input ref from receipt `state_refs` while preserving the denial diagnostic.
- 2026-06-10 Trellis actormap transaction slice: `RuntimeActormapTransactionState` should carry canonical sorted before/after/spawned/removed/visible/used object refs plus commit/rollback outcome. Commit requires `(before - removed) + spawned == after`, spawned visible after commit, and removed not used/visible; rollback requires after == before and spawned not visible. Register `molten.trellis-runtime.actormap-transaction.v1` in harness schema and keep test refs sorted via helper before evaluation.
- 2026-06-10 Trellis revocation cleanup slice: keep `RuntimeRevocationCleanupState` as public-field struct instead of a 6-arg constructor to satisfy Octet; validate sorted/unique canonical revoked and remaining refs, deny attempted reuse and any remaining assertion/subscription/pending-call/child ref that intersects the revoked set, and register `molten.trellis-runtime.revocation-cleanup.v1` for runtime receipt parsing.
- 2026-06-10 Trellis promise pipeline slice: model bounded forwarding locally with `RuntimePromisePipelineState`/entries, canonical target refs, strict increasing u64 sequence, terminal-promise queue cleanup, and `molten.trellis-runtime.promise-pipeline.v1` receipts. - 2026-06-08 retention GC apply-from-plan UX: `retention-gc-apply-v1` is stored under `retention/gc-applies` and requires a stored dry-run `plan_ref`; apply reads the plan, rejects requester/evidence mismatches, recomputes a fresh plan from the embedded candidate/evidence, runs normal destructive admission, denies without retention receipt/tombstone on denied/stale/drifted plans, and only then calls normal retention evaluation for unchanged passing plans. Re-apply after tombstone drifts because tombstone refs change the recomputed plan. Apply receipts are deletion-safety evidence only, not authority/policy/resource/provenance/transport/execution/source-gate/remote-GC trust. - 2026-06-08 retention bundle verify UX: `retention bundle-verify --bundle ...` emits `retention-candidate-bundle-verify-v1` with pass only when `bundle.preserves`, `explain.preserves`, and grouped local plan/apply/execute/audit/receipt/tombstone files are canonical, listed, and kind-correct; tampered, missing, duplicate, unlisted, or unreferenced files produce deny diagnostics. Verification is review evidence only and imports no trust.
- 2026-06-08 retention bundle export profiles: `retention bundle-export --profile internal|public|diagnostic` writes `retention-candidate-bundle-profile-v1`; public denies sensitive markers like `private-secret-ref`/`encrypted-ref`, diagnostic writes a redacted review view under `redacted/`, and profile receipts/redacted views do not replace verification or grant import/deletion trust.
- 2026-06-08 retention negative CLI matrix: integration coverage now exercises missing plan refs, stale/drifted plan apply, missing apply refs, wrong-scope apply refs, missing execution refs, and denied execution audits; ledger content remains readable for apply-gate denials.
- CLI integration tests work under nextest/Nix using `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_molten")`, `Command`, temp dirs under `std::env::temp_dir`, and parsing stdout/files back through `preserves_rail::parse_text`.
- Successful `molten test gate check` now emits canonical `<gate-receipt-v1 ...>` on stdout by default or to `--receipt-out`; tests should parse the Preserves receipt instead of expecting the old text `gate check ok` summary.
- 2026-05-31 Octet high-arity burn-down pattern works in small module slices: add prefix-free input structs, update all call sites/tests, run focused cargo checks, then refresh lib/workspace Octet artifacts before changing README counts.

## Patterns That Don't Work
- 2026-06-13 Nix dogfood after adding `src/cli_octet.rs`: local cargo passed, but `nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.dogfood-local-node` failed with `file not found for module cli_octet` because the new file was untracked and omitted from the flake source. Before Nix flake checks that depend on new files, temporarily `git add` new files (or otherwise ensure they are tracked in the Git source), then reset the index after validation if not committing yet.
- 2026-06-10 Cairn CLI path surprise: `/home/brittonr/git/cairn` was absent, `cairn` was not on PATH, `nix run .#cairn` was not provided by Molten, and `nix develop -c cairn` hit the repo `cairn/` directory shadow (`cannot execute: Is a directory`). Use the checked-out cargo manifest/policy path from the working pattern instead of assuming a local Cairn app exists.
- Running Cairn CLI without `--bin cairn` fails because `cairn-cli` has multiple binaries; running without `--policy` in this repo fails because Molten has no local `cairn-policy/generated/cairn-policy.json`.
- While implementing the first harness slice, `Cow<Vec<Value<IOValue>>>.iter()` yielded `Value<IOValue>` items for this use and `parse_step` needed an explicit borrow; also remove unused imports after `cargo fmt` expanded grouped imports.
- After adding `effect_sequence` to runtime-state hashing, tests with hard-coded final state refs silently failed to tamper the report because the hash changed. Use `run.final_state_hash` in tests instead of frozen refs unless the test is explicitly about a golden fixture.
- The `rg` tool pattern is passed through a shell; unescaped `|` can be interpreted as a pipeline and accidentally run other commands, and unquoted parentheses can also be interpreted by the shell. I repeated this mistake again on 2026-05-27, once more while checking testing-harness terms, while inspecting unit2nix, while checking test writes, again while checking `HARNESS_FAILURE_SCHEMA|failure`, on 2026-05-28 while searching optional admission helpers, again while searching `report_value\(|build_report_value\(`, again while searching `allow-all|compatibility|...`, and again while searching Nickel/Basalt policy terms; use `bash` with quoted `grep -E`/`rg '...'` or avoid shell metacharacters in `rg` patterns.
- Steel does not provide a top-level `(system ...)` binding; `(require-builtin steel/process)` loads but process API names need checking before using Steel as a shell substitute for validation commands.

## Domain Notes
- `molten` is a fresh Rust/Nix scaffold (not currently a git repo) with only `greeting()` implemented, but dependencies point toward Steel Scheme, Nickel, Wasmtime/WASI/WIT/component tooling, iroh blobs/docs/gossip, Syndicate, Preserves, Serde, Clap, Tracing, Blake3, Snafu, Redb, Hegel property testing, and OnixResearch Basalt/Cairn/Octet/Trellis.
- Trellis already exports verified choreography primitives: `choreography_global::{GlobalChoreo, GlobalBranch, well_formed_global}`, `choreography_local::LocalChoreo`, `choreography_projection::{projectable, project_endpoint}`, and `choreography_step` one-step global/local semantics. For Molten choreography design, prefer wrapping these rather than treating ChoRus as the normative protocol layer.
- Added Cairn change `trellis-raft-consensus` to scope Raft/Trellis to strongly consistent control-plane state (protocol registry, membership, capability/policy versions, receipt/replay ledgers), explicitly not normal actor messages, ordinary choreography steps, gossip, blobs, or local-only dataspace assertions.
- Added Cairn change `trellis-runtime-predicates` as follow-up for bounded Trellis predicates over local runtime invariants: assertion visibility/dedup/retraction, turn commit/rollback, Preserves pattern subset, Observe delivery, promise/pipeline state, revocation cleanup, actormap transactions, near/far refs, snapshot authority, service dependencies, and predicate receipt naming.
- Hegel Rust's package is `hegeltest`, the library crate is imported as `hegel`, and the source repo is `https://github.com/hegeldev/hegel-rust`.
- Cairn runtime-spine design now treats BEAM/OTP and Lunatic as non-normative design references only; do not claim BEAM distribution/OTP/Erlang/Elixir/Lunatic API compatibility.
- Runtime spine now has explicit requirement `molten.runtime_spine.preserves_comms`: all communication surfaces crossing runtime/trust/transport/execution/storage/policy/evidence boundaries need canonical Preserves representation; large payload bytes are referenced via Preserves metadata/content refs.
- Added Cairn change `synit-sam-runtime` for Synit/SAM-inspired local runtime semantics: turn commit/rollback, assertion lifetimes/auto-retraction, Observe patterns, deterministic Preserves patterns, capability attenuation, gatekeeper resolver, service dependency assertions, logical supervision, demand-driven startup, and canonical interaction tracing. Synit remains non-normative prior art, not compatibility target.
- Added Cairn change `goblins-vat-runtime` for Spritely Goblins-inspired object layer: vats/near-vs-far refs, transactional actormap, object-capability references, promises/vows and pipelining, revocable proxies, sealer/unsealer rights amplification, safe serialization/upgrade, time-travel debugging, OCapN concepts without wire compatibility, and portable encrypted storage principles. Correct framing: Molten's public model is SAM actors/entities/facets/dataspaces; vats are optional internal actor/service object territories, not the public actor model.
- Unison ideas worth adopting for Molten are patterns, not compatibility: content-addressed artifact/definition registry with names as metadata; ability/effect signatures as capability manifests with swappable handlers; remote closure/artifact dependency sync over Iroh under policy; typed durable storage via schema/type/artifact refs and upgrade recipes; structured refactor/upgrade sessions.
- Added and validated five Unison-inspired Cairn changes: `unison-artifact-registry`, `unison-effect-handlers`, `unison-remote-artifact-sync`, `unison-typed-storage`, and `unison-upgrade-sessions`. Validation command succeeded with 11 changes and 7 specs valid.
- Further Unison patterns worth considering for Molten: unique-vs-structural schema identity, dependency-closure evaluation/test caching, executable transcripts as first-class docs/bug repros, structured find/replace over canonical artifacts, project/branch/release metadata over artifact DAGs, Unison Share-like artifact catalog, MCP introspection tools, and lazy distributed job DAG memoization.
- Added and validated six more Unison-inspired Cairn changes: `unison-schema-identity`, `unison-evaluation-cache`, `unison-executable-transcripts`, `unison-structured-rewrite`, `unison-artifact-catalog-mcp`, and `unison-distributed-job-dag`. Validation command succeeded with 17 changes and 7 specs valid.
- Added `deterministic-test-playback` as a central Molten law: same artifacts/dependency closure/initial state/schema+policy refs/handler profile/seed or recorded effect log must reproduce identical canonical traces, receipts, outputs, and final state hash. Validation succeeded with 18 changes and 7 specs valid.
- Added and validated eight safety/lifecycle/ops Cairn changes: `authority-identity-revocation`, `resource-governance-backpressure`, `delivery-idempotency-replay`, `failure-supervision-lifecycle`, `retention-gc-pinning`, `secrets-redaction-confidentiality`, `supply-chain-provenance-builds`, and `peer-bootstrap-negotiation`. Validation succeeded with 26 changes and 7 specs valid.
- Added `content-addressed-chunk-store` Cairn for deterministic chunk manifests/Merkle roots, fixed_v1 chunking, streaming verification, resumable fetch, range reads, cross-object dedup, chunk-level retention/GC, and confidentiality-aware chunk refs. Validation succeeded with 27 changes and 7 specs valid.
- User says they own Aspen as well. Aspen is AGPL-3.0-or-later but owner can relicense owned code; still check third-party contributions/vendor code before copying into Molten MIT/Apache. Useful Aspen patterns for Molten: Iroh-only ALPN transport, Raft KV + blob/docs core layering, CAS coordination primitives with fencing/visibility/DLQ, operator receipts/dogfood-local, deterministic madsim artifacts, plugin host ABI/permissions/lifecycle, federation pull-sync with signed announcements/delegate signatures/content hashes, persistent Iroh identity, VM jobs by blob ref, blob-aware KV offload/replication.
- Added six Aspen-inspired Cairn changes using neutral names and SAM-compatible framing: `persistent-node-identity`, `operator-receipts-dogfood`, `plugin-host-abi`, `federated-pull-sync`, `blob-ref-job-submission`, and `coordination-primitives`. Validation succeeded with 33 changes and 7 specs valid.
- User clarified Molten is intended to be Aspen 2.0. Frame future architecture as retaining Aspen's operational lessons while replacing ad-hoc RPC/app layering with Molten's canonical Preserves envelope spine, SAM dataspace semantics, deterministic replay law, explicit policy/evidence, and artifact/chunk registry.
- Added and validated `first-class-testing-harness` Cairn. Key user points: (1) the testing harness needs a Preserves communication rail; harness control, actor stimuli, assertions/retractions, adapter fixtures, effect records, observations, traces, receipts, diagnostics, oracles, and reports must cross harness/runtime boundary as canonical Preserves values or Molten envelopes; (2) determinism and replay are core harness invariants: evidence-bearing runs are deterministic, replayed, recorded for replay, or explicitly non-replayable/excluded from deterministic gates; (3) added preflight guards for explicit harness privileges, hermetic inputs, schema/version discipline, fail-closed evidence, visible fixture mutation, production/test separation, secret hygiene, governed golden updates, resource/logical-time bounds, scheduler/liveness outcomes, adapter contract gates, and replay eligibility; (4) added rails for adapter conformance, cross-actor-kind interoperability (native Rust/Steel/Wasm/adapter-backed/remote-proxy actors communicate through the same envelope/dataspace/hostcall/policy/effect/trace/receipt boundaries), system-layer suites for Synit/SAM-like demand-driven services/supervision without Synit compatibility claims, repro bundles, counterexample shrinking, negative/security suites, upgrade replay, boundary coverage, deterministic multi-peer simulation, resource regression, golden traces, and flake prevention. Validation succeeded with 34 changes and 8 specs valid.
- Added and validated `haskell-runtime-patterns` Cairn: Haskell ideas are non-normative patterns only. Adopt pure core/effectful shell, capability-style effect handlers, Hegel property laws/shrinking, STM-style transactional turns, adapter law conformance, newtype/phantom-authority discipline, typed protocol states, golden canonical traces, parser-combinator-style deterministic DSLs, optic-inspired redaction/diffs, and strictness/resource guards. Validation succeeded with 35 changes and 9 specs valid.

- First implementation slice landed: `src/preserves_rail.rs` canonical Preserves packed bytes + blake3 refs, `src/harness.rs` deterministic fresh local native two-actor runner with observe/assert/send/clock/random/retract, report/replay validation, CLI in `src/main.rs`, and `examples/two-actor.preserves`. Follow-up added fail-closed report validation (`test report validate`), first trace-divergence diagnostics for tampered observations, and repro bundle stub (`test repro export`) writing report/suite/summary/refs. Then split harness boundaries into `src/harness/core.rs` (pure runtime transitions, no Preserves/IO), `schema.rs` (Preserves DTOs), `runner.rs`, and `replay.rs`; clock/random now emit explicit effect-request/effect-response records. Later added explicit `<effect-log-v1 "molten.harness.effect-log.v1" [...]>` report field; `test replay` now runs through recorded effect-log injection and compares request/response sequence, with tests for effect-request, effect-response, and effect-log divergence. Resource budget evidence (`<budget-v1 "molten.harness.budget.v1" ...>`) covers max steps/effects/events/report bytes; runner fails deterministically on budget exhaustion and validation checks budget usage matches observations/effect-log/canonical report bytes. Budgets are configurable in suite schema, old suite shape still defaults. Added actor registry evidence (`<actor-registry-v1 "molten.harness.actor-registry.v1" [<actor id kind>...]>`), reserved actor kinds native/steel/wasm/adapter/remote-proxy, validates unknown actors and currently rejects non-native kinds, and checks report actor registry matches embedded suite. Added canonical failure artifacts: `<harness-failure-v1 "molten.harness.failure.v1" <phase ...> <kind ...> <message ...> [...]>`, summary/show support, suite/report refs in diagnostics, divergence step/expected/actual/detail diagnostics, and CLI artifact emission to stdout by default or to file for `test run --report-out`, `test replay --failure-out`, `test report validate --failure-out`, and `test repro export --failure-out`. Nextest follow-ups: `.config/nextest.toml` with default/ci/deterministic/exploratory profiles, README commands, flake app `nextest-ci`, config check `nextest-config`, and real flake check `nextest` that builds unit2nix test binaries, synthesizes nextest metadata, and runs `cargo nextest run --profile ci`. Runtime-kernel follow-up extracted `src/runtime/` with canonical Preserves `RuntimeValue`, BTreeSet-backed messages/assertions/observers, minimal begin/commit/rollback turns, harness compatibility re-exports in `src/harness/core.rs`, and arbitrary Preserves values for send/observe/assert/retract payloads. Admission-gate follow-up added `src/runtime/admission.rs`, optional harness `<policy-v1 "molten.harness.policy.v1" [<deny actor action target value reason>...]>` where `#f` is wildcard, per-step canonical `<admission-decision-v1 ...>` trace events, denied turn rollback before commit, denied effect suppression (no effect-request/response), policy-decision replay divergence classification, and diagnostics tests. This follow-up was verified with `cargo test`, `cargo nextest run --profile ci`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `nix build .#checks.x86_64-linux.nextest --no-link --print-out-paths --option substituters https://cache.nixos.org/ --option builders ''`. Nix emits noisy `.links/... has maximum number of links` warnings locally but the checks pass. | 2026-05-30 | self | In `src/rewrites.rs`, imported `preserves::ValueImpl` and then used `.iter()` on `Vec<String>` fields; Rust resolved to Preserves value iteration, causing confusing `Iterator<Item=Value<_>>` errors. | In modules importing `ValueImpl`, use `.as_slice().iter()` for `Vec<String>`/ref vectors, or avoid importing `ValueImpl` where plain vector iteration is needed. |
| 2026-05-31 | self | Re-implemented a `required_u64` helper in `src/catalog.rs` as if `Value::as_u64()` returned `Option<u64>`; it actually returns `Option<Result<u64, OutOfRange>>`. | Copy the existing helper pattern: unwrap the option, then map the inner range error into `MoltenError`. |
| 2026-05-31 | self | Ran `cargo test node_runtime job_dag`, but Cargo accepts only one test filter before `--`; the second filter is an unexpected argument. | Run focused filters separately, use a broader single substring, or run full `cargo test`. |

- 2026-05-29: Started content-addressed chunk store implementation (`src/chunk_store.rs`). Fixed_v1 chunking uses domain-separated chunk hashes plus canonical Preserves chunk manifests/root refs; filesystem local store under chunks/manifests/metadata/pins; CLI under `molten test chunk put/verify/read/range/pin/gc`; ledger now classifies chunk manifests/receipts. Tests cover stable manifest identity, cross-object dedup, corrupted/missing chunk rejection, range reads, CLI lifecycle, and pinned-GC preservation. Validation so far: cargo test 117 total, fmt, clippy, nextest 117, Cairn strict 58/32. Remaining chunk-store Cairn tasks include Redb index, full resumable remote fetch/sync, exhaustive pin/unpin/tombstone/denial receipts, confidentiality/compression policy enforcement, Iroh adapter, typed-storage/replay/catalog integration, and property tests.
- 2026-05-29: `src/chunk_store.rs` already has filesystem-backed `sync_missing_chunks` and generic chunk-store receipts, but Cairn tasks remain open because there is no Redb availability/partial-fetch index, no full pin/unpin/tombstone/denial receipt coverage, and no Iroh/remote adapter yet. Best next slice is Redb local index first.
- 2026-05-29: Implemented Redb chunk-store index (`chunk-index.redb`) for manifests/chunks/availability/pins/partial fetches/receipts plus `index-status`/`index-rebuild`, pin/unpin/chunk-pin receipts, and sync partial-fetch state. Rebuild preserves historical receipt index entries while rebuilding derived manifest/chunk/availability/pin/partial-fetch tables. Redb gotcha: do not call `index_status()` while a `Database` handle for the same file is still alive (`Database already open. Cannot acquire lock`); explicitly `drop(db)` after rebuild commit before reopening. Validation: fmt, cargo test 119 total, clippy, nextest 119, Nix nextest `/nix/store/8f1z6sxf8i86pn7jqx5z01lnf9rclsss-molten-nextest`, Cairn strict 58/32.

- 2026-05-29: Bluefin Haskell docs add one high-value pattern for Molten: make effect/handler capabilities explicit value-level handles with handler-scoped lifetimes, so multiple same-kind effects are disambiguated by handle refs and operations cannot use handles outside their introducing scope. Treat as non-normative prior art; do not adopt Bluefin's API, Haskell phantom encoding, or unsafePerformIO implementation. Added Cairn change `explicit-effect-handle-scoping`; Cairn strict validate passed with 60 changes / 34 specs.

- 2026-06-01 Steel process runner pattern works: `(require-builtin steel/process)` + `(require-builtin steel/core/result)`, then `(spawn-process (command "cargo" '("check")))`, guard `Err?`, unwrap with `Ok->value`, and `(wait child)`. Do not use `(require "steel/core/result")` (wrong path). `spawn-process` here only takes one command argument; to set env vars for a child, invoke `env NAME=value command ...` rather than `#:env`.
